# AGMSG-TASK dotfiles-T133-worker-cap-hookspath-a02 — INV-6 reset of dotfiles-T133-worker-cap-hookspath-a01 (plan 006 S2; plan 024 P1-7)

This text is the independent redesign by a review-profile context other than the author of a01. Plan 006 INV-6 stopped a01 at S2 after three changes to its task text. The task-level audit of `c96697ae309afbe67527f53f5006fe24f055982a` returned `incorrect`. The earlier local review and worker report remain historical evidence.

PR #408 continues under this task on the same branch, `tools/worker-cap-hookspath-a01`. The implementation at `c96697ae309afbe67527f53f5006fe24f055982a` is the starting point. The next push replaces `.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-worker-cap-hookspath-a01/task.md` with this dispatched text's byte-identical copy at `.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-worker-cap-hookspath-a02/task.md`. The orchestrator handles removal of the predecessor task copy as reset bookkeeping; worker edits remain within `allowed_files`. The a01 acceptance history retains its stopped status and original counters.

The four premises preserve a01's write-time observations verbatim. Validation below records the redesigned final head separately. The reusable cap material originated at `9c07098147d3b16d68a9be2772fbcba2460b8851` on the stopped #393 branch.

## Goal

`herdr-agents --add-worker` enforces the deployed manifest's `worker_cap: 4`, with counting and registration under one exclusive lock, and the SKILL names that manifest cap. Ordinary main checkouts and main checkouts created with `git init --separate-git-dir` use the repository's actual common Git directory for the lock. This task introduces no hooks-path configuration and no Git configuration write.

## Files

1. `home/dot_agents/agent-config.yaml` contains `worker_cap: 4` immediately after `worker_worktree`, with the existing-style comment explaining the maximum number of seated worker worktrees and the deployed `~/.agents/agent-config.yaml` source. Other manifest values remain unchanged. `make render-check` and `make validate-agent-assets` accept the key without renderer or validator changes. A validator refusal produces a `status=blocked` PONG with the command and output.

2. `home/dot_local/bin/common/executable_herdr-agents` enforces the cap in add-worker mode after the main-checkout check and before `write_spawn_options`. A seat is a registered Git worktree under `.claude/worktrees/` holding a `claude-code` or `codex` agmsg identity; a worktree holding both counts once. Re-seating an existing seat adds none. The deployed manifest's cap uses the existing positive-digits-only `sed` parser. A missing or malformed key prints `no worker_cap in <path>; no cap enforced.` on stderr and continues. An unseated target with `seats >= cap` prints `worker_cap is <cap> and <n> worker seats are registered; remove one with --remove-worker first.` and exits 2 before any Herdr or agmsg write. One exclusive `flock` covers the count, `ensure_worker_identity`, `ensure_worker_worktree` and the registration completed by `spawn.sh`. A concurrent invocation waits and recounts after the first registration. The lock remains held through the spawn wait and is released afterwards. Item 6 defines its path.

   - When a RESULT arrives, run acceptance for that task while the others continue; acceptance follows RESULT arrival order.
   - A freed worker is re-tasked immediately with the next dependency-free task whose files overlap no in-flight task. Never leave a seated worker idle while a dispatchable task exists. The next task starts on a fresh branch from `origin/main`, and the previous task's branch stays in the worktree, untouched, for its revise rounds and until its acceptance. The worker commits and pushes everything before each RESULT, and before every branch switch it commits the newer task's work (or stashes it under a named tag and restores it afterwards), so a switch never carries edits across branches. When a `status=revise` arrives for the earlier task, it checks that branch out again, does the round, and returns to the newer task's branch, so the worktree is reused sequentially and nothing uncommitted is ever left behind.
   - When a merge moves `main`, every in-flight PR whose base moved, prose or code, merges the new base into its branch with `gh pr update-branch` before its CI, Bot wait and gate. The ruleset's strict up-to-date policy refuses the merge otherwise. `gh pr update-branch` creates a merge commit by default, not a rebase. A real conflict blocks only that PR.

+        threads = [threading.Thread(target=add, args=(name,), daemon=True) for name in ("concurrent-a", "concurrent-b")]
+        self.calls_path.write_text("")
+        common = Path(
+            subprocess.check_output(
+                ["git", "-C", str(main), "rev-parse", "--path-format=absolute", "--git-common-dir"], text=True
+            ).strip()
+        )
+        with (common / "herdr-agents-seat.lock").open("a") as lock:
+            fcntl.flock(lock, fcntl.LOCK_EX)
+            for thread in threads:
+                thread.start()
+            start.wait()
+            deadline = time.monotonic() + 10
+            while time.monotonic() < deadline and self.calls_path.read_text().count("main-check ") < 2:
+                time.sleep(0.01)
+            time.sleep(1)
+            held_calls = self.calls_path.read_text()
+            held_waiting = all(thread.is_alive() for thread in threads)
+            fcntl.flock(lock, fcntl.LOCK_UN)
+        for thread in threads:
+            thread.join(timeout=20)
+        self.assertFalse(any(thread.is_alive() for thread in threads))
+        self.assertGreaterEqual(held_calls.count("main-check "), 2, held_calls)
+        self.assertTrue(held_waiting)
+        self.assertNotIn("seat-read ", held_calls)
+        self.assertEqual(len(results), 2)
+        self.assertEqual(sorted(result.returncode for _, result in results), [0, 2], results)
+        [refused] = [result for _, result in results if result.returncode == 2]
+        self.assertIn("worker_cap is 4 and 4 worker seats are registered", refused.stderr)
+        calls = self.calls_path.read_text().splitlines()
+        self.assertEqual(sum(call.startswith("workspace create ") for call in calls), 1, calls)
+        self.assertEqual(sum(call.startswith("spawn ") for call in calls), 1, calls)
+        self.assertEqual(len(seated.read_text().splitlines()), 4)
+
+    def test_add_worker_uses_git_common_dir_for_the_seat_lock(self) -> None:
+        common = self.temp_dir / "separate.git"
+        subprocess.run(
+            ["git", "init", "-q", "--separate-git-dir", str(common), str(self.workdir)],
+            check=True,
+            capture_output=True,
+        )
+        self.write_worktree_seat(main_identities="dotfiles\tclaude-remediation-dot")
+        self.write_seat_lifecycle_fakes()
+        main = self.workdir.resolve()
+        self.assertTrue((main / ".git").is_file())
+        resolved = Path(
+            subprocess.check_output(
+                ["git", "-C", str(main), "rev-parse", "--path-format=absolute", "--git-common-dir"], text=True
+            ).strip()
+        )
+        self.assertTrue(resolved.is_absolute())
+        self.assertEqual(resolved, common.resolve())
+        (self.home_dir / ".agents/agent-config.yaml").write_text("worker_cap: 1\n")
+        scripts = self.home_dir / ".agents/skills/agmsg/scripts"
+        seated = scripts / "seated.tsv"
+        seated.write_text("")
+        (scripts / "identities.sh").write_text(
+            f"""#!/usr/bin/env bash
+if [[ $1 == {main} ]]; then
+    [[ $2 != claude-code ]] || printf 'dotfiles\\tclaude-remediation-dot\\n'
+    exit 0
+fi
+printf 'seat-read %s\\n' "$*" >> {self.calls_path}
+awk -F'\\t' -v p="$1" -v k="$2" '$1 == p && $2 == k {{ print $3 "\\t" $4 }}' {seated}
+"""
+        )
+        spawn = scripts / "spawn.sh"
+        spawn.write_text(spawn.read_text() + f'\nprintf "%s\\t%s\\t%s\\t%s\\n" "$4" "$1" "$6" "$2" >> {seated}\n')
+        real_git = shutil.which("git")
+        self.assertIsNotNone(real_git)
+        self.write_executable(
+            "git",
+            f"""#!/usr/bin/env bash
+if [[ $* == '-C {main} rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-dir' ]]; then
+    printf 'main-check\\n' >> {self.calls_path}
+fi
+exec {shlex.quote(real_git)} "$@"
+""",
+        )
+        results = []
+        thread = threading.Thread(
+            target=lambda: results.append(self.run_helper("--add-worker", ".claude/worktrees/separate")), daemon=True
+        )
+        self.calls_path.write_text("")
+        with (resolved / "herdr-agents-seat.lock").open("a") as lock:
+            fcntl.flock(lock, fcntl.LOCK_EX)
+            thread.start()
+            deadline = time.monotonic() + 10
+            while time.monotonic() < deadline and "main-check" not in self.calls_path.read_text():
+                time.sleep(0.01)
+            time.sleep(0.2)
+            held_calls = self.calls_path.read_text()
+            held_waiting = thread.is_alive()
+            fcntl.flock(lock, fcntl.LOCK_UN)
+        thread.join(timeout=20)
+        self.assertFalse(thread.is_alive())
+        self.assertEqual(len(results), 1)
+        self.assertEqual(results[0].returncode, 0, results[0].stdout + results[0].stderr)
+        self.assertIn("main-check", held_calls)
+        self.assertTrue(held_waiting)
+        self.assertNotIn("seat-read", held_calls)
+        self.assertNotIn("spawn ", held_calls)
+        self.assertEqual(len(seated.read_text().splitlines()), 1)
+        self.pane_list_path.write_text('{"result":{"panes":[]}}')
+        self.calls_path.write_text("")
+        refused = self.run_helper("--add-worker", ".claude/worktrees/excess")
+        self.assertEqual(refused.returncode, 2, refused.stdout + refused.stderr)
+        self.assertIn("worker_cap is 1 and 1 worker seats are registered", refused.stderr)
+        self.assertFalse((main / ".claude/worktrees/excess").exists())
+        self.assertNotIn("spawn ", self.calls_path.read_text())

+    def test_add_worker_refuses_a_seat_beyond_the_manifest_worker_cap(self) -> None:
+        self.write_worktree_seat(main_identities="dotfiles\tclaude-remediation-dot")
+        self.write_seat_lifecycle_fakes()
+        scripts, main = self.home_dir / ".agents/skills/agmsg/scripts", self.workdir.resolve()
+        manifest = self.home_dir / ".agents/agent-config.yaml"
+        for value in ("", "worker_cap: invalid\n"):
+            with self.subTest(manifest=value):
+                manifest.write_text(value)
+                self.pane_list_path.write_text('{"result":{"panes":[]}}')
+                self.calls_path.write_text("")
+                result = self.run_helper("--add-worker", ".claude/worktrees/s0")
+                self.assertEqual(result.returncode, 0, result.stdout + result.stderr)
+                self.assertIn(f"no worker_cap in {manifest}; no cap enforced.", result.stderr)
+                self.assertIn(f"workspace create --cwd {main}/.claude/worktrees/s0 ", self.calls_path.read_text())
+        manifest.write_text("worker_kind: claude\nworker_cap: 4\n")
+        rows = []
+        for n in range(1, 5):
+            path = self.add_seat_worktree(f"s{n}")
+            kind = "claude-code" if n % 2 else "codex"
+            rows.append(f"{path}\t{kind}\tdotfiles\tworker-a00{n}\n")
+        seated = scripts / "seated.tsv"
+        seated.write_text("".join(rows))
+        (scripts / "identities.sh").write_text(
+            f"""#!/usr/bin/env bash
+printf 'identities %s\\n' "$*" >> {self.calls_path}
+if [[ $1 == {main} ]]; then
+    [[ $2 != claude-code ]] || printf 'dotfiles\\tclaude-remediation-dot\\n'
+    exit 0
+fi
+awk -F'\\t' -v p="$1" -v k="$2" '$1 == p && $2 == k {{ print $3 "\\t" $4 }}' {seated}
+"""
+        )
+        self.pane_list_path.write_text('{"result":{"panes":[]}}')
+        self.calls_path.write_text("")
+
+        result = self.run_helper("--add-worker", ".claude/worktrees/s5")
+
+        self.assertEqual(result.returncode, 2, result.stdout + result.stderr)
+        self.assertIn(
+            "worker_cap is 4 and 4 worker seats are registered; remove one with --remove-worker first.", result.stderr
+        )
+        self.assertFalse((main / ".claude/worktrees/s5").exists())
+        calls = self.calls_path.read_text().splitlines()
+        self.assertFalse(
+            any(c.startswith(("join ", "spawn ", "delivery ", "workspace create", "tab create")) for c in calls)
+        )
+        self.calls_path.write_text("")
+        result = self.run_helper("--add-worker", ".claude/worktrees/s1")
+        self.assertEqual(result.returncode, 0, result.stdout + result.stderr)
+        self.assertNotIn("worker_cap", result.stderr)
+        self.assertIn(f"workspace create --cwd {main}/.claude/worktrees/s1 ", self.calls_path.read_text())
+
+    def test_add_worker_writes_no_git_configuration(self) -> None:
+        self.write_worktree_seat(main_identities="dotfiles\tclaude-remediation-dot")
+        self.write_seat_lifecycle_fakes()
+        main = self.workdir.resolve()
+        worktree = main / ".claude/worktrees/hooks"
+        git_calls = self.temp_dir / "git-calls.txt"
+        real_git = shutil.which("git")
+        self.assertIsNotNone(real_git)
+        self.write_executable(
+            "git", f'#!/usr/bin/env bash\nprintf "%s\\n" "$*" >> {git_calls}\nexec {shlex.quote(real_git)} "$@"\n'
+        )
+
+        result = self.run_helper("--add-worker", ".claude/worktrees/hooks")
+
+        self.assertEqual(result.returncode, 0, result.stdout + result.stderr)
+        calls = git_calls.read_text().splitlines()
+        self.assertFalse(any(" config " in call for call in calls), calls)
+        for directory in (main, worktree):
+            for key in ("extensions.worktreeConfig", "core.hooksPath"):
+                config = subprocess.run(
+                    [real_git, "-C", str(directory), "config", "--get", key],
+                    check=False,
+                    text=True,
+                    capture_output=True,
+                )
+                self.assertEqual(config.returncode, 1, config.stdout + config.stderr)
+                self.assertEqual(config.stdout, "")
+
+    def test_the_skill_names_the_manifest_worker_cap(self) -> None:
+        [cap] = re.findall(
+            r"^worker_cap: ([0-9]+)$", (ROOT / "home/dot_agents/agent-config.yaml").read_text(), re.MULTILINE
+        )
+        skill = (ROOT / "home/dot_agents/skills/agmsg-orchestration/SKILL.md").read_text()
+        counts = [line for line in skill.splitlines() if "at most `worker_cap` workers" in line]
+        self.assertEqual(len(counts), 2, counts)
+        for line in counts:
+            self.assertIn(f"({cap} in the manifest today", line)
+        self.assertNotRegex(skill, r"about three concurrent workers|at most three workers")
+

21:- A Claude session started in the repository root outside Herdr (a plain shell, mosh/ssh, or `claude -p`) is a pane-less orchestrator: nothing seats a worker automatically, and the SessionStart `herdr-agents --attach` hook prints a summary line naming that state, the on-demand commands, and any seated worker, followed in a regime repository by the `agmsg-orchestration:` directive line. Bring the regime up on demand: claim the seat outside the sandbox with the composite instance id, `actas-claim.sh <repo> claude-code <name> <session_id>.<claude pid>` (a claim from sandboxed Bash writes the bare session id and turn delivery then skips silently; see the seat-lock bullet below); seat the worker with `herdr-agents --add-worker <worktree>` (it derives `HERDR_SOCKET_PATH` from the default Herdr server socket `~/.config/herdr/herdr.sock`, the path the Claude sandbox allowlists, before creating anything, accepts a claude worker's workspace-trust dialog during spawn's readiness wait, and takes `--ready-timeout <seconds>`); confirm the worker's placement from its `run/spawn.*` placement record itself (the `herdr:<socket>:<pane>` row that upstream `agmsg_spawn_path` resolves) and the `linkage=` line `--add-worker` prints, never from `team.sh <team> --json`, which observes Codex members by reading their pane; treat the `linkage=` line `--add-worker` prints as the `AGMSG-PING` evidence, and on `pong=no` or `linkage=unreached` wake again with `agmsg-dispatch <team> <orchestrator> <worker> <pane> 'AGMSG-PING v1 task_id=<id> reason=<reason>'` and verify the PING's `read_at` (`poke.sh` only for a viewed workspace); and dispatch no AGMSG-TASK before its `AGMSG-PONG` arrives. Without a managed workspace the auditor runs headless, in the form the task-level audit bullet below names. A pane-less session uses turn delivery when the Monitor cannot run; see "Identity, delivery, and storage" for the platform limits.
33:- Delegate all repository-mutating work — file edits, builds, test runs, and git state changes — to seated workers, with at most one seated worker per git worktree and sequential assignments within one worktree. Add worktrees for parallelism; never use parallel `codex exec` or per-task Codex spawning, except that the read-only, non-interactive task-level audit the orchestrator runs during acceptance (the task-level audit bullet below) is not worker spawning and is permitted; it is identity-less. agmsg/herdr control-plane commands (`delivery.sh`, `watch.sh`, `actas-claim.sh`, `send.sh`, `join.sh`, and herdr agent/pane commands) are orchestrator-side exemptions.
43:  - Some steps stay sequential. The single audit tab serializes audits; the task-level audit of T67 reduces them to one per task. The orchestrator-side gate (`make require-crit-review`) and merges also run one at a time.
72:- Before every acceptance action (revise, accept, merge) the orchestrator runs `scripts/regime-counters.sh <task id>` and pastes its output into the acceptance record. S2 is decided from INV-6's counts: each component the script prints is read from its output, and for each component it prints as `unknown` the record pastes the command and output that read the source INV-6 names for that component, or, while that source has no entry for the task, says so and pastes the reading of the interim record the orchestrator used in its place. S1 (plan 006: the same finding class three times on one artifact, from any reviewer) is decided from the record's finding-class table, which gives a class to every finding the PR-feedback sweep lists, whatever its disposition, and to every task-level audit finding; three rows of one class stop the artifact. Read that table over non-advisory rows only: list advisory P2 rows with their classes (`advisory-p2` and the `local:<sha7>:<n>` rows `local-advisory.sh` writes), exclude them from S1's three-rows count, and name the excluded rows in the record. The gate's S6 check still sees every ledger row, so an advisory class at three rows gets its `searches` entry from `acceptance-front-matter.py` like any other. INV-6's message and head counts and the S2 lines (`revises`, `amendments`, `questions`, `bot_p1_heads`) take no ledger rows; `scripts/regime-counters.sh` prints them unchanged.
170:       - A P2 finding of the Codex Bot, local review or task-level audit is advisory: disposition it `not-applicable:advisory P2 owned by <lineage>-followup-a0<n>` and record an `<id><TAB><class>` row under `## Owned by <owner>` in the lineage's acceptance record, with the orchestrator writing the owner task file before the row.
173:    2. Run the task-level audit of that head in the pair or headless form of the task-level audit bullet. It exits nonzero for every verdict other than `correct`. An exit with `Audit verdict: incorrect` is not a failed step: continue to step 3 and disposition its findings. A `blocked` or missing verdict means re-running the audit.
174:    3. Write the acceptance record, summarising the dispositions, with every audit finding dispositioned as the task-level audit bullet says: for an `incorrect` verdict the record's JSON `audit_findings`, which the gate reads through `ACCEPTANCE_EVIDENCE`.

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	
     3	# @file herdr-agents
     4	# @brief Start the Claude Code orchestrator in Herdr and seat workers on demand.
     5	# @description
     6	#   Full mode creates or heals an agents workspace with the orchestrator pane
     7	#   only and never creates a second workspace for a directory that already has
     8	#   one; it never starts a worker. Attach mode, the SessionStart hook in the
     9	#   orchestrator pane, claims the orchestrator seat and prints the directive
    10	#   without starting or repairing any worker; outside a Herdr pane it only
    11	#   prints a bring-up summary line (and, in a regime repository, the directive
    12	#   line). Workers are seated on demand with --add-worker in their own tab of
    13	#   the orchestrator's workspace and removed with --remove-worker;
    14	#   --restart-worker is retired and exits 2. Audit
    15	#   mode runs the read-only Codex audit of one commit visibly in the
    16	#   workspace's dedicated `audit` tab, tees it to an evidence file, and waits
    17	#   (bounded) for its exit marker, then gates on the concluding `Verdict:` line
    18	#   of its `-o` last-message file; the auditor keeps no agmsg identity.
    19	#   Before the gate it masks the evidence with DIR/scripts/validate-agent-assets.py
    20	#   --mask-secrets. DIR must be the orchestrator's own checkout (the audited
    21	#   commit is only fetched): the masker is refused, and the audit fails as
    22	#   `unmasked`, when DIR is at the audited commit or the validator is missing
    23	#   though git tracks it, untracked, or changed, and a failed mask also fails.
    24	#   Masking is skipped only when git tracks no validator and none is on disk.
    25	#   Starting the orchestrator pane, and the SessionStart --attach hook inside
    26	#   it, claim the orchestrator's agmsg seat outside the sandbox under the
    27	#   composite `<session_id>.<claude pid>` instance id (`seat_claim=` line),
    28	#   followed in a regime repository by the `agmsg-orchestration:` directive
    29	#   line. agmsg bootstrap also removes the pre-push stub that earlier versions
    30	#   wrote for the retired main-push guard; the GitHub ruleset on `main` is the
    31	#   boundary.
    32	#   A codex worker (an --add-worker seat) is launched with
    33	#   `--ask-for-approval never` and `sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true`,
    34	#   so it never prompts and out-of-sandbox actions fail instead of escalating.
    35	#   The orchestrator pane starts Claude with the
    36	#   `MODEL_PROFILE_<NAME>_CLAUDE_ARGS` of the profile that
    37	#   MODEL_PROFILE_INTERACTIVE names in ~/.agents/model-profiles.env.
    38	# @option --attach Attach the current Claude pane to its Herdr workspace layout.
    39	# @option --restart-worker Retired: exits 2 naming --remove-worker then --add-worker.
    40	# @option --bootstrap-agmsg Configure repo-scoped agmsg hooks without changing Herdr panes.
    41	# @option --audit <sha> Run the read-only `codex <audit profile args> exec` auditor for <sha> in the workspace's audit tab.
    42	# @option --out <path> Audit evidence path, relative to DIR. Defaults to
    43	#   `.orchestration/validation/audit-<sha>.md`.
    44	# @option --timeout <seconds> Audit exit-marker wait bound. Defaults to 1800.
    45	# @option --task <id> Audit the task once on its final head <sha>: the prompt names
    46	#   `.orchestration/tasks/<id>.md`, the worker's report, validation and sandbox
    47	#   files and `<id>-pr-feedback.json` (those present), and the full PR diff from
    48	#   `git merge-base origin/main <sha>`. Defaults --out to
    49	#   `.orchestration/validation/<id>-audit-<sha7>.md`.
    50	# @option --add-worker [<worktree>] Seat a worker for DIR/<worktree> via agmsg spawn.sh; the
    51	#   worktree defaults to the manifest worker_worktree when omitted.
    52	#   The deployed manifest's worker_cap limits seated worker worktrees.
    53	# @option --remove-worker <worktree> Despawn that worker and close its tab (or its own workspace).
    54	# @option --kind <codex|claude> Add-worker agent kind. Defaults to the manifest worker_kind.
    55	# @option --profile <name> Add-worker model profile. Defaults to the manifest worker_profile.
    56	# @option --ready-timeout <seconds> Add-worker: spawn.sh readiness wait bound (spawn.sh default 90).
    57	# @option --force Remove-worker: tear down a dirty worktree's worker and despawn with --force.
    58	# @option --stale Remove-worker: retire a seat whose pane is gone (no despawn; delivery off, leave, the placement record moved to the run directory's stale/); refused for any other state.
    59	# @arg DIR Optional directory for the Herdr workspace. Defaults to the current directory.
    60	# @arg HERDR_AGENTS_WORKER_KIND Add-worker agent kind, `codex` or `claude`. Defaults
    61	#   to `worker_kind` from the manifest via ~/.agents/model-profiles.env, then
    62	#   `codex`.
    63	# @arg HERDR_AGENTS_WORKER_PROFILE Environment variable naming the worker's
    64	#   model profile: `--profile <name>` for a codex worker, or the profile whose
    65	#   `MODEL_PROFILE_<NAME>_CLAUDE_ARGS` supplies arguments for a claude worker.
    66	#   Defaults to `worker_profile` from the manifest via
    67	#   ~/.agents/model-profiles.env, then MODEL_PROFILE_INTERACTIVE from the same
    68	#   file, then standard.
    69	# @arg HERDR_AGENTS_CLAUDE_ARGS Optional space-delimited Claude arguments for
    70	#   manifest-sourced E2E profile overrides on the orchestrator pane, appended
    71	#   after the interactive profile args. Defaults to no arguments.
    72	# @example
    73	#   herdr-agents ~/Workspace/dotfiles
    74	# @example
    75	#   herdr-agents --attach
    76	# @example
    77	#   herdr-agents --add-worker .claude/worktrees/worker-c --kind claude
    78	# @example
    79	#   herdr-agents --bootstrap-agmsg ~/Workspace/dotfiles
    80	# @example
    81	#   herdr-agents --audit 926d9f1 --out .orchestration/validation/T31-audit.md ~/Workspace/dotfiles
    82

    85	# @description Print usage information.
    86	function usage() {
    87	    cat << 'USAGE'
    88	Usage: herdr-agents [DIR]
    89	       herdr-agents --attach
    90	       herdr-agents --bootstrap-agmsg [DIR]
    91	       herdr-agents --audit <sha> [--task ID] [--out PATH] [--timeout SECONDS] [DIR]
    92	       herdr-agents --add-worker [<worktree>] [--kind codex|claude] [--profile NAME] [--ready-timeout SECONDS] [DIR]
    93	       herdr-agents --remove-worker <worktree> [--force] [--stale] [DIR]
    94	       herdr-agents --directive
    95	
    96	Create a Herdr workspace for DIR with the Claude Code orchestrator pane only,
    97	and open DIR in Zed when available; workers are seated on demand with
    98	--add-worker. Herdr, jq and Claude Code are required. DIR defaults to the
    99	current directory.
   100	Full mode heals an existing managed workspace for DIR instead of creating a
   101	second one (it restarts a missing orchestrator, never a worker), and exits 2
   102	when more than one managed workspace exists.
   103	Attach mode uses the current Herdr pane for Claude: it claims the orchestrator
   104	seat and prints the directive, and never starts, restarts or repairs a worker.
   105	Outside a Herdr pane it changes nothing and prints a summary line: the
   106	orchestrator pane is not started, the on-demand worker and auditor commands,
   107	and the manifest worktree's seated worker, if any. In a regime repository (a
   108	main checkout with one orchestrator agmsg identity and a manifest worker
   109	worktree) an agmsg-orchestration directive line follows, as it follows
   110	seat_claim= inside the orchestrator's Herdr pane.
   111	Full and attach modes seat a Claude orchestrator, so they exit 2 before
   112	touching Herdr when HERDR_AGENTS_ORCHESTRATOR_KIND (default orchestrator_kind
   113	from ~/.agents/model-profiles.env, then claude) is codex; the other modes work
   114	under either kind (the worker modes name, link and despawn workers under the
   115	kind's orchestrator identity), and a worker's own attach in a linked worktree
   260	# @exitcode 2 If the path exists but is not a worktree of this repository, or cannot be created.
   261	function ensure_worker_worktree() {
   262	    local workdir="$1"
   263	    local path="$1/$2"
   264	    local listed
   265	
   266	    if [[ -e ${path} ]]; then
   267	        path="$(cd -- "${path}" && pwd -P)"
   268	        listed="$(git -C "${workdir}" worktree list --porcelain 2> /dev/null | sed -n 's/^worktree //p')"
   269	        if ! grep -Fxq -- "${path}" <<< "${listed}"; then
   270	            printf 'herdr-agents: %s exists but is not a worktree of %s; refusing to seat the worker there.\n' "${path}" "${workdir}" >&2
   271	            exit 2
   272	        fi
   273	    elif ! git -C "${workdir}" worktree add --detach "${path}" origin/main > /dev/null 2>&1; then
   274	        printf 'herdr-agents: unable to create worker worktree %s from origin/main in %s.\n' "${path}" "${workdir}" >&2
   275	        exit 2
   276	    else
   277	        path="$(cd -- "${path}" && pwd -P)"
   278	    fi
   279	    printf '%s\n' "${path}"
   280	}
   281	
   282	# @description Print `<team><TAB><name>` of the agmsg identity seated at a worker
   283	#   worktree, registering one when none exists. An existing single registration
   284	#   there is reused. A new one is <kind>-<profile>-<suffix>-aNNN (next free NNN)
   285	#   in the orchestrator's team, where team and suffix come from the
   286	#   orchestrator's one non-worker (no -aNNN) claude-code identity at the main
   287	#   checkout; it is joined with AGMSG_RESOLVE_PROJECT=0 so upstream project
   288	#   resolution (#92) cannot rewrite the worktree path to the main checkout,
   289	#   unless $4 is `--no-join` (spawn.sh joins it itself).
   290	# @arg $1 string Worker kind.
   291	# @arg $2 workdir Absolute main checkout path.
   292	# @arg $3 path Absolute worker worktree path.
   293	# @arg $4 string Optional `--no-join` to only derive the identity.
   294	# @exitcode 2 If the worktree or orchestrator registration is ambiguous.
   295	function ensure_worker_identity() {
   296	    local kind="$1"
   297	    local workdir="$2"
   298	    local worktree="$3"
   299	    local join="${4:-}"
   300	    local scripts="${HOME}/.agents/skills/agmsg/scripts"
   301	    local agent_type seated orchestrator team suffix name next
   302	
   303	    agent_type="$(worker_agmsg_type "${kind}")"
   304	    if [[ ! -x ${scripts}/identities.sh || ! -x ${scripts}/join.sh ]]; then
   305	        printf 'agmsg scripts not found; skipping worker identity registration: %s\n' "${scripts}" >&2
   306	        return 0
   307	    fi
   308	    seated="$(AGMSG_RESOLVE_PROJECT=0 "${scripts}/identities.sh" "${worktree}" "${agent_type}" 2> /dev/null | sort -u)" || seated=""
   309	    # One name in several teams is one seat (distinct names decide, as in
   310	    # distinct_agmsg_identity_count).
   311	    if [[ "$(cut -f 2 <<< "${seated}" | sort -u | grep -c .)" -gt 1 ]]; then
   312	        printf 'herdr-agents: several agmsg %s identities are registered at %s (%s); refusing to pick one.\n' "${agent_type}" "${worktree}" "$(cut -f 2 <<< "${seated}" | sort -u | tr '\n' ' ' | sed 's/ $//')" >&2
   313	        exit 2
   314	    fi
   315	    if [[ -n ${seated} ]]; then
   316	        head -n 1 <<< "${seated}"
   317	        return 0
   318	    fi
   319	    orchestrator="$(AGMSG_RESOLVE_PROJECT=0 "${scripts}/identities.sh" "${workdir}" "${orchestrator_agmsg_type}" 2> /dev/null |
   320	        awk -F '\t' '$2 !~ /-a[0-9][0-9][0-9]$/' | sort -u)" || orchestrator=""
   321	    if [[ -z ${orchestrator} || ${orchestrator} == *$'\n'* ]]; then
   322	        printf 'herdr-agents: need exactly one orchestrator %s identity at %s to name the worker; register the worker yourself with: AGMSG_RESOLVE_PROJECT=0 %s/join.sh <team> <name> %s %q\n' \
   323	            "${orchestrator_agmsg_type}" "${workdir}" "${scripts}" "${agent_type}" "${worktree}" >&2
   324	        exit 2
   325	    fi
   326	    team="${orchestrator%%$'\t'*}"
   327	    suffix="${orchestrator##*-}"
   328	    next="$(AGMSG_RESOLVE_PROJECT=0 "${scripts}/team.sh" "${team}" --json 2> /dev/null |
   329	        jq -r '.[]?.member // empty' | sed -n 's/.*-a\([0-9][0-9][0-9]\)$/\1/p' | sort -n | tail -n 1)" || next=""
   330	    printf -v name '%s-%s-%s-a%03d' "${kind}" "${HERDR_AGENTS_WORKER_PROFILE}" "${suffix}" "$((10#${next:-0} + 1))"
   331	    if [[ ${join} != --no-join ]]; then
   332	        AGMSG_RESOLVE_PROJECT=0 "${scripts}/join.sh" "${team}" "${name}" "${agent_type}" "${worktree}" > /dev/null
   333	    fi
   334	    printf '%s\t%s\n' "${team}" "${name}"
   335	}
   336	
   337	# @description Point agmsg delivery at the worker worktree when its hook is
   338	#   missing: `both` for claude-code (turn delivery; upstream session-start.sh
   339	#   skips sessions under .claude/worktrees, #367, so no Monitor watch starts
   340	#   there), `turn` for codex. delivery.sh bakes the path into the hook.
   341	# @arg $1 string Worker kind.
   342	# @arg $2 path Absolute worker worktree path.
   343	function ensure_worker_delivery() {
   344	    local kind="$1"
   345	    local worktree="$2"
   346	    local delivery="${HOME}/.agents/skills/agmsg/scripts/delivery.sh"
   347	    local log_file="${HOME}/.config/herdr/herdr-agents.log"
   348	
   349	    [[ -x ${delivery} ]] || return 0
   350	    mkdir -p "${log_file%/*}"
   351	    if [[ ${kind} == claude ]]; then
   352	        jq -e 'any(.hooks.Stop[]?.hooks[]?; ((.command // "") | contains("agmsg/scripts/check-inbox.sh")))' \
   353	            "${worktree}/.claude/settings.local.json" > /dev/null 2>&1 && return 0
   354	        "${delivery}" set both claude-code "${worktree}" >> "${log_file}" 2>&1 || true
   355	    else
   356	        jq -e 'any(.hooks.Stop[]?.hooks[]?; ((.bash // .command // "") | contains("agmsg/scripts/check-inbox.sh")))' \
   357	            "${worktree}/.codex/hooks.json" > /dev/null 2>&1 && return 0
   358	        if "${delivery}" set turn codex "${worktree}" >> "${log_file}" 2>&1; then
   359	            printf 'Codex loads the new hook in %s only after that project .codex layer is trusted; run /hooks or trust it in Codex.\n' "${worktree}" >&2
   360	        fi
   361	    fi
   362	}
   363	
   364	# @description Add merge-denial rules to a Claude worker worktree's local
   365	#   settings, preserving hooks and other permissions. Main checkouts and Codex
   366	#   workers are untouched.
   367	# @arg $1 string Worker kind.
   368	# @arg $2 path Absolute worker worktree path.
   369	function ensure_worker_merge_denials() {
   370	    local kind="$1" worktree="$2"
   371	    local settings="${worktree}/.claude/settings.local.json" input temporary
   372	
   373	    [[ ${kind} == claude && -f ${worktree}/.git ]] || return 0
   374	    is_main_checkout "${worktree}" && return 0
   375	    mkdir -p "${settings%/*}"
   376	    input="${settings}"
   377	    [[ -f ${input} ]] || input=/dev/null
   378	    temporary="$(mktemp "${settings}.XXXXXX")"
   379	    jq -s '.[0] // {} | .permissions.deny = ((.permissions.deny // []) as $deny |
   380	        $deny + (["Bash(gh pr merge:*)", "Bash(gh api -X PUT:*)",
   381	                  "Bash(gh api --method PUT:*)", "Bash(gh api graphql:*)"] - $deny))' \
   382	        "${input}" > "${temporary}"
   383	    if cmp -s "${settings}" "${temporary}"; then
   384	        rm -- "${temporary}"
   385	    else
   386	        mv -- "${temporary}" "${settings}"
   387	    fi
   388	}
   389	
   390	# @description Print the Codex `-c` override that makes a linked worktree's git
   391	#   metadata writable for a codex worker. A worktree's index, HEAD and objects
   392	#   live under the main checkout's git common dir, outside the workspace-write
   393	#   root, so every git add/commit/fetch/rebase would otherwise fail (the worker
   394	#   runs with --ask-for-approval never, so nothing escalates). Granted:
   395	#   <common>/objects, <common>/refs, <common>/logs and the worktree's own
   396	#   <common>/worktrees/<name>; the common
   397	#   dir itself, config, hooks, info, HEAD, packed-refs and, in a shallow
   398	#   clone, shallow (so git fetch --deepen/--unshallow still fails, reported on
   399	#   stderr) stay read-only.
   400	#   `-c` replaces the array, so the roots configured in
   401	#   ${CODEX_HOME:-~/.codex}/config.toml (the agmsg store) are kept in front.
   402	#   The file is parsed with python3's tomllib (3.11+). The grant fails closed:
   403	#   when the file exists but cannot be parsed, or its writable_roots is not a
   404	#   list of strings, it prints a stderr line and no override, so the worker
   405	#   keeps its configured roots. Prints nothing for a main checkout (its git dir
   406	#   is the common dir).
   407	# @arg $1 path Worker worktree.
   408	# @stdout `sandbox_workspace_write.writable_roots=[...]`, or nothing.

  1787	        # driver refuses without it; derive the default server socket before

# @exitcode 2 If the path is missing or not a worktree of this repository.
function repo_worktree_path() {
    local path

    if ! path="$(cd -- "$1/$2" 2> /dev/null && pwd -P)" ||
        ! git -C "$1" worktree list --porcelain 2> /dev/null | sed -n 's/^worktree //p' | grep -Fxq -- "${path}"; then
        printf 'herdr-agents: %s is not a worktree of %s.\n' "$1/$2" "$1" >&2
        exit 2
    fi
    printf '%s\n' "${path}"
}

# @description Succeed when DIR is a git main checkout (not a linked worktree).
# @arg $1 workdir Absolute directory.
function is_main_checkout() {
    local git_dir common_dir

    git_dir="$(git -C "$1" rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-dir 2> /dev/null)" &&
        common_dir="$(git -C "$1" rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir 2> /dev/null)" &&
        [[ ${git_dir} == "${common_dir}" ]]
}

# @description Succeed when DIR is its repository's manifest worker_worktree seat.
# @arg $1 dir Absolute directory to check.
function is_manifest_worker_seat() {
    local dir="$1" seat common_dir

    seat="$(resolve_worker_worktree 2> /dev/null)" && [[ -n ${seat} ]] || return 1
    common_dir="$(git -C "${dir}" rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir 2> /dev/null)" || return 1
    [[ "$(cd -- "${common_dir%/.git}/${seat}" 2> /dev/null && pwd -P)" == "${dir}" ]]
}

        # A lone argument outside .claude/worktrees/ is DIR; an omitted

            set -- "${seat_worktree}"
            seat_worktree=""
        fi
        [[ -n ${seat_worktree} ]] || seat_worktree="$(resolve_worker_worktree)"
    fi

        self.agent_start_not_ready_path = self.temp_dir / "agent-start-not-ready.txt"

        self.agent_start_name_taken_path = self.temp_dir / "agent-start-name-taken.txt"

        self.process_info_state_path = self.temp_dir / "process-info-state.txt"
        self.orchestrator_session_path = self.temp_dir / "orchestrator-session.txt"
        # 1 makes the visible snapshot stale: it shows old transcript text and

        self.visible_stale_path = self.temp_dir / "visible-stale.txt"
        # The recent-unwrapped snapshot text.

        self.tab_list_path = self.temp_dir / "tab-list.json"

        self.pane_counter_path.write_text("2\n")

        self.write_executable(

            f"""#!/usr/bin/env bash
printf '%s\\n' "$*" >> {self.calls_path}

fi

fi
if [[ $1 == pane && $2 == split ]]; then
    workspace="${{3%%:*}}"

    printf '{{"id":"cli:pane:split","result":{{"pane":{{"pane_id":"%s:p%s"}}}}}}\\n' "$workspace" "$pane_number"
    exit 0
fi
if [[ $1 == pane && $2 == swap ]]; then
    exit 0
fi
if [[ $1 == pane && $2 == resize ]]; then
    if [[ -s {self.pane_layout_after_resize_path} ]]; then
        cp {self.pane_layout_after_resize_path} {self.pane_layout_path}
    fi
    exit 0

# AGMSG-TASK dotfiles-T133-worker-cap-hookspath-a01 — the numeric worker cap and the worktree hooks path (plan 024 P1-7)

Written 2026-10-18 by the orchestrator seat from plan 024 (wave P-W1 brought forward to P-W0 after D3 branched to (d), `024-todo.md` row P1-7, `024-design-programs.md` item (9), decision D-024-10: cap 4 now, 6 once spark's second seat runs). Seat: codex-standard-dot-a010 (worker-e). The SKILL says three, the regime runs four; the launcher enforces nothing. Routing: `herdr-agents` and the manifest's worker keys are launch surface, not a seat's execution boundary; a Codex seat edits them. The material is PR #393's branch `tools/integration-seat-result-gate-cap` at 9c07098147d3b16d68a9be2772fbcba2460b8851 (stopped under D4): reuse its cap check and tests, leave its integration seat, pre-RESULT gate and receipt verifier (owned by P1-4 and P1-5).

## Goal

`herdr-agents --add-worker` refuses a seat beyond the manifest's `worker_cap`, every worktree it seats runs the repository's `.githooks`, and the SKILL states the cap as the manifest's number.

## Files

1. `home/dot_agents/agent-config.yaml`: after `worker_worktree`, the key `worker_cap: 4` with a comment in the file's style (the number of seated worker worktrees `herdr-agents --add-worker` allows; read from the deployed `~/.agents/agent-config.yaml`; D-024-10). Nothing else; `make render-check` and `make validate-agent-assets` stay green without a renderer or validator change (confirm and paste; a refusal of the key by the validator is a `status=blocked` PONG, not a validator edit).
2. `home/dot_local/bin/common/executable_herdr-agents`, add-worker mode, after the main-checkout check and before `write_spawn_options`: the cap check of 9c07098 lines 1830 to 1848 (Amendment 1: not byte for byte; the count and the registration that follows run under one exclusive `flock` on `${workdir}/.git/herdr-agents-seat.lock`, held from the count through `ensure_worker_identity` and `ensure_worker_worktree`, so a concurrent add-worker waits and recounts) (a seat is a `.claude/worktrees/` worktree holding a claude-code or codex agmsg identity; re-seating a seated worktree adds none; `worker_cap` read with the digits-only `sed`; no key prints `no worker_cap in <path>; no cap enforced.` on stderr and continues; `seats >= cap` for an unseated worktree prints `worker_cap is <cap> and <n> worker seats are registered; remove one with --remove-worker first.` and exits 2 before any Herdr or agmsg write).
3. (Superseded by Amendment 2; the original text is kept for the record.) Same script, after `ensure_worker_worktree` returns the seat directory: `git -C "${workdir}" config --bool extensions.worktreeConfig true` when `git -C "${workdir}" config --get extensions.worktreeConfig` is not `true` (once per repository; the common config is the host's to write), then `git -C "${seat_dir}" config --worktree core.hooksPath .githooks` (per worktree; the main checkout's hooks are untouched; a missing `.githooks` directory runs no hook, and P1-5 adds the hooks). Verify both commands in a temporary repository before relying on them and paste the proof (`git config --worktree` needs the extension; `git -C <wt> config --get core.hooksPath` prints `.githooks` in the worktree and nothing in the main checkout). `--remove-worker` leaves the setting (the worktree is removed with it). The header's add-worker description gains one sentence for the cap and one for the hooks path.

## Orchestrator Playbook

1. Join or confirm the agmsg team and identities with the `agmsg` scripts.
2. Create the `.orchestration` directories before assigning work.
3. Write a task file that includes objective, scope, allowed files, forbidden actions, expected artifacts, validation commands, and max turns. Name the render check as `make render-check`, never the bare `python3 scripts/generate-agent-configs.py --check`, which fails without PyYAML. Ground `allowed_files` by grepping the repository for every touch point the task names (tests that pin call sequences, mirrors, fixtures) before dispatch. Verify every CLI constraint the task asserts by running the real command in a safe form, not by reading `--help`. Presume an auditor finding that contradicts your own review is right until you refute it with evidence. Decide the worker kind from the allowed files before dispatch: a seat never edits the source of its own execution boundary, so a change is routed by the boundary it touches. A change that touches only Claude's boundary goes to a Codex seat: the `claude.permissions`, `claude.sandbox` and `claude.hooks` blocks of `home/dot_agents/agent-config.yaml` (including excludedCommands, allowUnsandboxedCommands, writable roots, network and the PermissionRequest hook), the Claude-rendering parts of `scripts/generate-agent-configs.py`, the rendered `home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-settings-managed.json`, and `home/dot_claude/modify_private_settings.json`. A change that touches only Codex's boundary goes to a Claude seat: `codex.sandbox_workspace_write`, `codex.approval_policy`, the Codex-rendering parts of the renderer, and `home/.chezmoitemplates/codex-config-managed.toml`. A change that touches both, or a shared source the orchestrator cannot split (such as `codex.sandbox_workspace_write.writable_roots`, which also renders into Claude's `allowWrite`), goes to the operator. The orchestrator decides the kind from the diff the task will produce and records it and the chosen worker profile in the task file. The Claude Code auto-mode classifier also refuses a Claude-boundary task on a Claude seat as Self-Modification. A task that edits permgate (`home/dot_agents/permgate-policy.yaml`, `home/dot_local/bin/common/executable_permgate`), the PermissionRequest hook of both seats, goes to the operator; a Codex `security`-profile worker reviews the change before the orchestrator accepts it, per the model-selection rule. `auto` is Claude Code's built-in starting permission mode since 2.1.283. A project-level `defaultMode: auto` (`.claude/settings.json` or `.claude/settings.local.json`) does not take effect, and the session then also ignores the user-level `defaultMode`, so `auto` belongs only in user or managed settings. Before dispatch, read the task's verbatim blocks against each other for contradictions, and state each rule once; a second artifact references the first instead of restating it.
4. Start or relaunch worker panes only through `herdr-agents` modes. Deliver messages and wakes as in step 6; never use `pane send-text` followed by `send-keys Enter`, because the separate Enter races the TUI composer and fails nondeterministically.
5. Configure delivery deliberately. `delivery.sh set turn` is useful for turn-end inbox checks; changing delivery mode can kill project watcher processes, so do it before starting long-running project watchers.
6. Send `AGMSG-TASK v1` with the exact artifact paths and `done_signal=AGMSG-RESULT`. Pass every `send.sh`/`poke.sh` body with `--body-file <path>` (both take it in agmsg v1.5.0), never as a positional argument: a positional `<text>` passes through the caller's own shell first, where a backtick or `$( )` in the body silently executes and vanishes from what arrives (upstream #378). `agmsg-dispatch` is the one exception and takes a single-line, shell-safe positional message. Pick the path by how the recipient is seated, never by inferring pane or agent status: a worker in a `herdr-agents` pane gets `agmsg-dispatch <team> <from> <to> <pane_id> "<message>"` (send, generic wake, `read_at` wait; single-line shell-safe text only), the sanctioned wake path until worker seating writes placement records at launch, because `poke.sh` exits 1 with "no placement record" for a hand-joined member, and a herdr-agents worker gets a record only once it acts from its own pane (upstream `send.sh`/`inbox.sh` record the acting pane, #1109); a spawn-seated member (placement record `run/spawn.*`; `team.sh <team> --json` shows its pane) gets `poke.sh <team> <name> --body-file <path> [--retries N --retry-delay SECONDS --backoff fixed|exponential]`, which types and submits in one call and refuses to type over an in-progress draft (#1321/#1322); a pane-less member gets `send.sh <team> <from> <to> --body-file <path>` and its own delivery mode. Verify delivery via the messages.db `read_at` column. `poke.sh` exit codes: 10 = terminal unreachable, 12 = pane gone or no live agent, 14/15 = refused to type over a changing or unlocatable input box, 13 = the driver has no poke path for this pane (for example a `plain` terminal); where poke.sh's narrow agmsg-message fallback succeeds it exits 0, so 13 means nothing was delivered, and the printed reason names the native channel, asks the caller to claim its own identity first, or reports that the fallback failed. Never retry a 13 as `send.sh` yourself: that overrides the driver's considered refusal and can double-deliver. Seating case: a worker in an unviewed or headless Herdr workspace (no client attached, a small pane rect such as 18x41) makes `poke.sh` exit 14/15 from its TUI input locator; that is a locator refusal, not evidence about the worker's state, so wake it with `agmsg-dispatch` and verify `read_at`. Every dispatch (TASK, ACCEPTANCE, PING) is recorded in the current acceptance record under a `dispatch:` key as the bus line with its read marker, so the orchestrator creates that record, `.orchestration/acceptance/<task>.md`, before the task's first dispatch (`scripts/orchestrator/dispatch.sh` refuses a record that does not exist or that names another task); a seat is reported as working only from such a line, and after a context compaction the open TASK list is re-derived from a bus read that is refused when it reaches its limit (`bus-read.sh` exits when the history reaches `BUS_READ_LIMIT`; the orchestrator raises the limit and reads again), never from a default-limited one and never from a read whose truncation went unnoticed. A seated worker idle for 20 minutes since its latest bus event for the task while a dispatchable task exists (INV-10's idle figure; its 30 minutes is the first-push deadline) gets a bus read, then a PING; a longer silent wait is never the answer. `scripts/orchestrator/dispatch.sh` writes the line and `scripts/orchestrator/bus-read.sh` performs the read and prints the minutes since each open task's latest event (both on main since a03c1daa, plan 007 T718); the orchestrator applies the 20-minute figure to the minutes the script prints (the script's own silence flag still uses 30 minutes, a recorded residual of the tools lineage).
7. Track `max_turns`. Use `AGMSG-PING` for liveness if a worker stalls.
8. On `AGMSG-RESULT`, read the task file and every referenced artifact before deciding.
9. For a RESULT carrying `effects`, verify that every declared effect has the report's stated reverse mapping before acceptance; record any irreversible effect in the acceptance note.
10. For a RESULT that carries a pull request, integrate in this order. This is the single procedure that the PR integration rule, `AGENTS.md`, the README and the gh-first-workflow skill cite. A `gh pr update-branch` moves the head, so steps 1 to 4 run again on the new head. After an update-branch the orchestrator runs it, the order is CI on the new head, then the sweep, then the audit; an audit started before CI finishes sees incomplete evidence and returns an evidence-only `incorrect`. Select a checkout with git -C <absolute path>, never with cd, which the sandboxed Bash may not honour. After moving the review worktree to the audited head, verify git -C <review> rev-parse HEAD equals that head and git -C <main> symbolic-ref --short HEAD prints main before the audit and the gate.
    1. Sweep the final head's feedback with `scripts/pr-feedback.py <pr> --json .orchestration/validation/<task>-pr-feedback.json` and give every item a `fixed:<commit>` or `not-applicable:<reason>` disposition, leaving none on `failure` or `warning` annotations. Optionally request `@coderabbitai full review` on the final head first; when a CodeRabbit review exists it is swept like any other item, and the gate does not require a bot review. An `open:<thread id>` or `open:review-<review id>` item surviving the worker's fix round follows Worker Playbook step 15's operator-decision path; the PR is not merged, never with the thread open.
       - A P2 finding of the Codex Bot, local review or task-level audit is advisory: disposition it `not-applicable:advisory P2 owned by <lineage>-followup-a0<n>` and record an `<id><TAB><class>` row under `## Owned by <owner>` in the lineage's acceptance record, with the orchestrator writing the owner task file before the row.
       - Except that a small P2 on the PR's own change whose fix stays within the task's `allowed_files` is fixed in a revise round sent by the orchestrator with a `check=` while INV-6's counts allow it.
       - The post-round audit of the final head is the single authority on the PR's findings, dispositioned finding by finding before the gate; a P0/P1 it leaves is the operator's decision, and the merge follows sub-step 5.
    2. Run the task-level audit of that head in the pair or headless form of the task-level audit bullet. It exits nonzero for every verdict other than `correct`. An exit with `Audit verdict: incorrect` is not a failed step: continue to step 3 and disposition its findings. A `blocked` or missing verdict means re-running the audit.
    3. Write the acceptance record, summarising the dispositions, with every audit finding dispositioned as the task-level audit bullet says: for an `incorrect` verdict the record's JSON `audit_findings`, which the gate reads through `ACCEPTANCE_EVIDENCE`.
    4. Run the gate: `AUDIT_EVIDENCE=<audit> AGENT_REVIEWED=1 REVIEW_EVIDENCE=<receipt> ACCEPTANCE_EVIDENCE=<acceptance record> BASE=origin/main PR_FEEDBACK_EVIDENCE=<json> make require-crit-review`.
       - A `review` sweep item whose body carries a `P0`–`P3` badge is a finding with its own `fixed:<commit>` or `not-applicable:<reason>` disposition, never a container for its inline threads.
       - The sweep covers issue comments, reviews, inline review comments with thread resolution, non-passing check runs, check-run annotations at every level (`notice`, `warning`, `failure`), and commit statuses. A `failure` marked `not-applicable` needs a concrete reason of at least 20 characters.
       - A CodeRabbit full review is optional, at most once on the final head: the plan allows one review per hour and every review event spends one (docs.coderabbit.ai/management/rate-limits).
       - The feedback JSON may be masked with `uv run --no-project --with pyyaml scripts/validate-agent-assets.py --mask-secrets`, which masks its keys and string values; the gate identifies an item by its source, url, level, path, line and body, and accepts a body or path that is verbatim or exactly that masked form.
       - The gate rejects evidence outside `.orchestration/validation/` or without the `-pr-feedback.json` suffix. It binds `BASE` to the PR's GitHub base before running the collector from that authenticated SHA: an older base must be outside HEAD's first-parent chain, an advanced base must preserve the merge-base with the PR head, and PR branch commits (including `HEAD`) cannot substitute for the base. Evidence must match the local GitHub repository independently of `GH_REPO`, and `fixed:` commits must be in the authenticated GitHub base-to-head range whatever `BASE` is selected.
       - `AUDIT_EVIDENCE` must be the task-level file `.orchestration/validation/<task>-audit-<sha7>.md` (the same `<task>` as the feedback JSON); a per-commit `audit-<sha>.md` is rejected. Its verdict comes only from the non-empty `<file>.last.md` and must be `correct`, or `incorrect` with an `audit_findings` entry in the acceptance record for each of its findings. PRs that change only `.orchestration/` files need no audit.
       - A boundary PR (`orchestration/boundary-<date>[-n]`, `.orchestration` files only) skips the gate, the sweep JSON and the audit (with `BASE` set the gate would demand `PR_FEEDBACK_EVIDENCE`); each Bot thread on it still gets a disposition reply and is resolved, and the next boundary commit message names the PR.
    5. After the required checks pass and the threads are resolved, merge with `gh pr merge <pr> --squash --match-head-commit <audited head sha>`, so a newer head can never be merged on the evidence of the audited one. Every seat acts as the machine's one GitHub account, so no approval is required or possible. Who merges is decided by the integration gate and by native denial of merge commands in Codex seats; `herdr-agents` writes the Claude worker deny rules (`Bash(gh pr merge:*)`, `Bash(gh api -X PUT:*)`, `Bash(gh api --method PUT:*)`, `Bash(gh api graphql:*)`) into the worker worktree's `.claude/settings.local.json`, where [deny rules take precedence over allow rules and cover nested subcommands in every permission mode](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions), but a method flag after the path escapes these prefix rules, so the integration gate remains the authority.
    6. Send `AGMSG-ACCEPTANCE` (step 11).
11. Send `AGMSG-ACCEPTANCE v1 status=accepted` when done, or `status=revise` with a narrow `reason` and `next_action` when more work is required.

## Worker Playbook

1. Read the full `AGMSG-TASK v1` message.
2. Switch to the `repo` and read `task_file` before editing or running validations. Create the task branch without touching the shared `.git/config`, which is read-only for a Codex seat by design (T64 writable roots): `git switch -c <branch> --no-track origin/main`, push with `git push origin <branch>` (no `-u`), and open the PR as a draft with `gh pr create --draft --head <branch>`. Commit inside the sandbox, with `git -c commit.gpgsign=false` when the signing key is unreadable there; for a Claude seat only the push leaves the sandbox, and a Codex seat pushes from inside it (step 4). A Claude seat's sandbox can leave the same failed write behind, so it uses the same commands. A leftover `.git/config.lock` from a failed attempt is removed by the orchestrator. Remove a scratch worktree (for example one that proves a test fails on `origin/main`) with `git worktree remove <path>` only; never run `git worktree prune` from a sandboxed seat, because other worktrees' paths look missing inside the sandbox and prune then targets their admin directories in the shared `.git/worktrees`.
3. Treat `allowed_files` as the edit boundary. If it says to see the task file, read that section and follow it exactly.
4. Do not perform any `forbidden_actions`. Complete every command inside the sandbox and allowlist; never escalate an action outside that boundary for approval. Fail it instead, send `AGMSG-PONG v1 status=blocked` with the exact command and the boundary it crosses, and wait for the orchestrator to re-task. Agent-to-agent permission approval is forbidden: only the human operator answers a permission prompt. A Claude Code auto-mode classifier denial (for example Self-Modification) is such a boundary: stop without a diff, send `AGMSG-PONG v1 status=blocked` naming the classifier reason, and never pursue the same outcome through another tool. Fetch and fast-forward inside the sandbox; a public remote needs no credential. For Claude seats on macOS, Seatbelt denies the Mach lookup of `com.apple.trustd.agent`, so Go-based CLIs (`gh`, `gcloud`, `terraform`) fail TLS verification with `x509: OSStatus -26276` (`errSecInternal`); `git` uses Apple libcurl, LibreSSL and `/etc/ssl/cert.pem` and succeeds. On Linux, the bubblewrap sandbox denies the AF_UNIX keyring socket, so `gh` answers HTTP 401; `allowUnixSockets` cannot grant a path. Monitor and process visibility limits are in "Identity, delivery, and storage". On both platforms a Claude seat runs three commands outside the sandbox through the permission gate: `gh`, `git push`, and an authenticated `git fetch` (a private HTTPS remote, whose credential helper is `gh`); `excludedCommands` and `enableWeakerNetworkIsolation` are operator options, not selected alternatives. Look up a VERIFY item's external reference (upstream documentation, release notes, an API schema) with the WebFetch tool, not Bash `curl`. Three documented cases run outside the sandbox too: the task's main-checkout CompactionDB `memory add`, through the permission gate because that state directory is read-only from a worktree sandbox; writing the task's artifacts at their expected main-checkout paths and masking them with the repository masker, through the permission gate because the main checkout is not writable from a worktree sandbox; and `agmsg-dispatch`, through `excludedCommands` (step 11). Every other out-of-sandbox action stays a blocked PONG. A Codex seat has in-sandbox network (T64) and never leaves its sandbox.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

# List (team, agent) pairs registered for a given (project_path, agent_type).
#
# Usage: identities.sh <project_path> <agent_type>
#
# Output: one "<team>\t<agent>" line per registered pair, tab-separated.
# Empty output (and exit 0) when no pair matches. Pairs are deduplicated.
#
# Used by:
#   - whoami.sh        — exact-match enumeration for identity resolution
#   - watch.sh         — subscription set for the monitor delivery mode
#   - check-inbox.sh   — turn-mode fallback enumeration

PROJECT_PATH="${1:?Usage: identities.sh <project_path> <agent_type>}"
AGENT_TYPE="${2:?Missing agent_type}"
AGENT_TYPE_SQL=$(printf '%s' "$AGENT_TYPE" | sed "s/'/''/g")

SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
SKILL_DIR="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/.." && pwd)"  # resolve-project.sh requires SKILL_DIR
TEAMS_DIR="$SCRIPT_DIR/../teams"
# shellcheck disable=SC1091
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/resolve-project.sh"
# shellcheck disable=SC1091
source "$SCRIPT_DIR/lib/storage.sh"
PROJECT_SQL_IN=$(agmsg_project_sql_in_list "$PROJECT_PATH")

[ -d "$TEAMS_DIR" ] || exit 0

for config_file in "$TEAMS_DIR"/*/config.json; do
  [ -f "$config_file" ] || continue
  cfg_sql=$(agmsg_sql_readfile_path "$config_file")
  TEAM_NAME=$(agmsg_sqlite_mem "
    WITH raw(json) AS (SELECT CAST(readfile('$cfg_sql') AS TEXT)),
    cfg(json) AS (SELECT CASE WHEN json_valid(json) THEN json END FROM raw)
    SELECT json_extract(json, '\$.name') FROM cfg;
  ")
  [ -z "$TEAM_NAME" ] && continue
  [ "$TEAM_NAME" = "null" ] && continue
  TEAM_SQL=$(printf '%s' "$TEAM_NAME" | sed "s/'/''/g")

  sqlite3 -separator $'\t' :memory: "
    WITH raw(json) AS (SELECT CAST(readfile('$cfg_sql') AS TEXT)),
    cfg(json) AS (SELECT CASE WHEN json_valid(json) THEN json END FROM raw),
    agents AS (
      SELECT
        key AS name,
        CASE
          WHEN json_type(json_extract(value, '\$.registrations')) = 'array' THEN json_extract(value, '\$.registrations')
          ELSE json_array(json_object('type', json_extract(value, '\$.type'), 'project', json_extract(value, '\$.project')))
        END AS registrations
      FROM cfg, json_each(json_extract(cfg.json, '\$.agents'))
    )
    SELECT DISTINCT '$TEAM_SQL' AS team, name
    FROM agents, json_each(agents.registrations) AS r
    WHERE json_extract(r.value, '\$.project') IN ($PROJECT_SQL_IN)
      AND json_extract(r.value, '\$.type') = '$AGENT_TYPE_SQL'
    ORDER BY team, name;
  " | tr -d '\r'
done

# AGMSG-TASK dotfiles-T133-worker-cap-hookspath-a01 — the numeric worker cap and the worktree hooks path (plan 024 P1-7)

function codex_worktree_writable_roots() {
    local worktree="$1"
    local common git_dir config configured="[]"

    [[ -n ${worktree} ]] || return 0
    common="$(git -C "${worktree}" rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir 2> /dev/null)" || return 0
    git_dir="$(git -C "${worktree}" rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-dir 2> /dev/null)" || return 0
    [[ ${git_dir} == "${common}/worktrees/"* ]] || return 0
    config="${CODEX_HOME:-${HOME}/.codex}/config.toml"
    # A missing file or key leaves the configured roots empty, which -c cannot
    # narrow; any other doubt keeps the configured roots by emitting nothing.
    if [[ -e ${config} ]] && ! configured="$(
        python3 - "${config}" 2> /dev/null << 'PY'
import json
import sys
import tomllib

with open(sys.argv[1], "rb") as handle:
    roots = tomllib.load(handle).get("sandbox_workspace_write", {}).get("writable_roots", [])
if not isinstance(roots, list) or not all(isinstance(root, str) for root in roots):
    sys.exit(1)
print(json.dumps(roots))
PY
    )"; then
        printf 'herdr-agents: cannot read sandbox_workspace_write.writable_roots in %s as a list of strings (python3 3.11+ tomllib); the codex worker in %s gets no git metadata roots.\n' "${config}" "${worktree}" >&2
        return 0
    fi
    # The spawn options dialect strips ` #...` as a comment, so no root may hold `#`.
    if ! jq -cn --argjson configured "${configured}" '$configured + $ARGS.positional |
        if all(.[]; type == "string" and (contains("#") | not)) then "sandbox_workspace_write.writable_roots=" + tojson else error("unsupported") end' \
        -r --args "${common}/objects" "${common}/refs" "${common}/logs" "${git_dir}" 2> /dev/null; then
        printf 'herdr-agents: a writable root for the codex worker in %s contains "#"; it gets no git metadata roots.\n' "${worktree}" >&2
        return 0
    fi
    if [[ "$(git -C "${worktree}" rev-parse --is-shallow-repository 2> /dev/null)" == true ]]; then
        printf 'herdr-agents: %s is a shallow clone; its shallow metadata (%s/shallow) is not granted, so git fetch --deepen or --unshallow in the codex worker fails.\n' "${worktree}" "${common}" >&2
    fi
}

# @description Print the agmsg spawn options YAML that carries a worker
#   profile's launch arguments (spawn.sh splices the type section into the boot
#   command): MODEL_PROFILE_<NAME>_CLAUDE_ARGS for claude, and `--profile <name>
#   --sandbox workspace-write --ask-for-approval never --config
#   sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true` for codex, plus the
#   worktree's git metadata roots (`--config`, see
#   codex_worktree_writable_roots) for a codex worker when a worktree is given.
# @arg $1 string Worker kind.
# @arg $2 path Worker worktree (optional).
# @exitcode 2 If the profile is not defined in ~/.agents/model-profiles.env or
#   its arguments are not plain `--flag value` pairs.
function write_spawn_options() {
    local kind="$1"
    local profile_env_key args index roots
    local -a words=()

    profile_env_key="MODEL_PROFILE_$(printf '%s' "${HERDR_AGENTS_WORKER_PROFILE}" | tr '[:lower:]' '[:upper:]')_$(printf '%s' "${kind}" | tr '[:lower:]' '[:upper:]')_ARGS"
    args="$(
        # shellcheck source=/dev/null
        [[ ! -f ${HOME}/.agents/model-profiles.env ]] || source "${HOME}/.agents/model-profiles.env"
        printf '%s' "${!profile_env_key:-}"
    )"
    if [[ -z ${args} ]]; then
        printf 'herdr-agents: model profile %q is not defined (%s is unset in ~/.agents/model-profiles.env).\n' "${HERDR_AGENTS_WORKER_PROFILE}" "${profile_env_key}" >&2
        exit 2
    fi
#   seat_orchestrator_labels and seat_worker_labels (JSON arrays of
#   `<team>:<name>`).
# @arg $1 workdir Absolute directory.
function load_seat_labels() {
    local scripts="${HOME}/.agents/skills/agmsg/scripts"
    local main="$1" common rows worker_type seat_worktree

    seat_orchestrator_labels='[]'
    seat_worker_labels='[]'
    # $HOME is never an agmsg project (see bootstrap_agmsg).
    [[ "$(cd -- "$1" && pwd -P)" != "$(cd -- "${HOME}" && pwd -P)" ]] || return 0
    [[ -x ${scripts}/identities.sh ]] || return 0
    if common="$(git -C "$1" rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir 2> /dev/null)" &&
        [[ ${common} == */.git && -d ${common%/.git} ]]; then
        main="$(cd -- "${common%/.git}" && pwd -P)"
    fi
    rows="$(AGMSG_RESOLVE_PROJECT=0 "${scripts}/identities.sh" "${main}" claude-code 2> /dev/null |
        awk -F '\t' 'NF == 2 && $2 !~ /-a[0-9][0-9][0-9]$/' | sort -u)" || rows=""
    [[ -n ${rows} ]] || return 0
    seat_orchestrator_labels="$(jq -Rnc '[inputs | split("\t") | "\(.[0]):\(.[1])"]' <<< "${rows}")"
    worker_type="$(worker_agmsg_type "${worker_kind:-$(resolve_worker_kind)}")"
    seat_worktree="$(
        # shellcheck source=/dev/null
        [[ ! -f ${HOME}/.agents/model-profiles.env ]] || source "${HOME}/.agents/model-profiles.env"
        printf '%s' "${HERDR_AGENTS_WORKER_WORKTREE:-}"
    )"
    rows="$(AGMSG_RESOLVE_PROJECT=0 "${scripts}/identities.sh" "${main}" "${worker_type}" 2> /dev/null |
        jq -Rr --argjson orchestrators "${seat_orchestrator_labels}" \
            'split("\t") | select(length == 2) | select(("\(.[0]):\(.[1])") as $label | $orchestrators | index($label) | not) | join("\t")')" || rows=""
    if [[ ${seat_worktree} =~ ^\.claude/worktrees/[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$ && -d ${main}/${seat_worktree} ]]; then
        rows+=$'\n'"$(AGMSG_RESOLVE_PROJECT=0 "${scripts}/identities.sh" "${main}/${seat_worktree}" "${worker_type}" 2> /dev/null)" || true
    fi
    seat_worker_labels="$(jq -Rnc '[inputs | split("\t") | select(length == 2) | "\(.[0]):\(.[1])"] | unique' <<< "${rows}")"
}

# @description Map self-named seat pane labels on stdin pane-list JSON back to
#   the pair roles: `<team>:<orchestrator>` to `claude-orchestrator` and the
#   worker seat's `<team>:<name>` to `<kind>-worker`. The panes keep their real
#   labels in herdr; only herdr-agents' view changes.
function normalize_seat_labels() {
    jq -c --argjson orchestrators "${seat_orchestrator_labels:-[]}" --argjson workers "${seat_worker_labels:-[]}" \
        --arg worker "${worker_kind:-$(resolve_worker_kind)}-worker" \
        'if (.result.panes | type) == "array" then
             .result.panes |= map((.label // "") as $label
                 | if ($orchestrators | index($label)) then .label = "claude-orchestrator"
                   elif ($workers | index($label)) then .label = $worker
    local panes_json

    workspace_list_json="$(herdr workspace list)"
    while IFS=$'\t' read -r workspace_id workspace_label; do
        [[ -n ${workspace_id} ]] || continue
        if ! panes_json="$(managed_pane_list "${workspace_id}")"; then
            continue
        fi
        if printf '%s\n' "${panes_json}" | jq -e --arg cwd "${workdir}" --arg label "${label}" --arg workspace_label "${workspace_label}" \
            '.result.panes[]? | select(.cwd == $cwd and ($workspace_label == $label or .label == "claude-orchestrator"))' > /dev/null; then
            printf '%s\n' "${workspace_id}"
        fi
    done < <(printf '%s\n' "${workspace_list_json}" | jq -r '.result.workspaces[]? | select(.workspace_id) | [.workspace_id, (.label // "")] | @tsv')
}

# @description Print the single managed workspace id for a workdir.
# @arg $1 label Full-mode Herdr workspace label.
# @arg $2 workdir Absolute workdir path.
# @exitcode 2 If more than one managed workspace exists for workdir.
function single_managed_workspace() {
    local workspace_ids

    workspace_ids="$(find_managed_workspaces "$1" "$2")"
    if [[ ${workspace_ids} == *$'\n'* ]]; then
        printf 'herdr-agents: multiple managed Herdr workspaces for %q (%s); the orchestrator and its worker tabs live in one workspace. Close the stray one: herdr agent prompt <pane> "/exit" for each of its agents, then herdr workspace close <id>.\n' \
            "$2" "$(tr '\n' ' ' <<< "${workspace_ids}" | sed 's/ $//')" >&2
        exit 2
    fi
    printf '%s\n' "${workspace_ids}"
}

    def run_helper(self, *mode: str, extra_env: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:
        env = os.environ.copy()
        env["HOME"] = str(self.home_dir)
        env["PATH"] = f"{self.bin_dir}{os.pathsep}/usr/bin{os.pathsep}/bin"
        env.pop("HERDR_AGENTS_WORKER_PROFILE", None)
        env.pop("HERDR_AGENTS_WORKER_KIND", None)
        env.pop("HERDR_AGENTS_CLAUDE_ARGS", None)
        env.pop("HERDR_AGENTS_NAME_RELEASE_POLLS", None)
        env.pop("HERDR_AGENTS_NAME_RELEASE_INTERVAL", None)
        env.pop("FPATH", None)
        env.pop("CODEX_HOME", None)
        env["HERDR_SOCKET_PATH"] = str(self.temp_dir / "herdr.sock")
        env.pop("CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID", None)
        env.pop("CLAUDE_PID", None)
        # The default socket path honours XDG_CONFIG_HOME, which CI runners set.
        env.pop("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", None)
        env["HERDR_AGENTS_LINKAGE_PONG_WAIT"] = "0"
        if extra_env:
            env.update(extra_env)
        return subprocess.run(
            ["bash", str(SCRIPT), *mode, str(self.workdir)],
            cwd=ROOT,
            env=env,
            check=False,
            text=True,
            stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
            stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
        )

    def write_worktree_seat(

            "claude-remediation-dot",
            "claude-standard-dot-a005",
            "claude-standard-dot-a006",
        ),
    ) -> Path:
        """A git repo with origin/main, a manifest worker_worktree, and path-aware agmsg fakes."""
        for args in (
            ("init", "-q"),
            ("-c", "user.name=t", "-c", "user.email=t@example.invalid", "commit", "-q", "--allow-empty", "-m", "init"),
            ("update-ref", "refs/remotes/origin/main", "HEAD"),
        ):
            subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(self.workdir), *args], check=True, capture_output=True)
        profiles = self.home_dir / ".agents/model-profiles.env"

        profiles.write_text(
            'HERDR_AGENTS_WORKER_KIND="claude"\n'
            'HERDR_AGENTS_WORKER_PROFILE="standard"\n'
            'HERDR_AGENTS_WORKER_WORKTREE=".claude/worktrees/worker-c"\n'
            'MODEL_PROFILE_STANDARD_CLAUDE_ARGS="--model opus --effort high"\n'
            'MODEL_PROFILE_STANDARD_CODEX_ARGS="--profile standard"\n'
            'MODEL_PROFILE_REVIEW_CODEX_ARGS="--profile review"\n'
        )
        scripts = self.home_dir / ".agents/skills/agmsg/scripts"

case "$1" in
{self.workdir.resolve()}/.claude/worktrees/*) [[ $2 != claude-code ]] || cat {scripts / "at-worktree.txt"} ;;
{self.workdir.resolve()}) [[ $2 != claude-code ]] || cat {scripts / "at-main.txt"} ;;
esac
exit 0
""",

""",

""",

        pong: bool = False,
        pong_task_id: str = "",
        spawn_panes: tuple[str, ...] = ("w-test:p9",),
    ) -> Path:
        """Fake agmsg spawn/despawn/leave on top of the worktree-seat fakes.

        spawn.sh places pane w-test:p9 (the workspace then lists spawn_panes); a fake agmsg-dispatch on PATH records
        the add-worker linkage PING (read, optionally answered by a PONG) in a
        temporary messages.db that fake lib/storage.sh resolves.
        """
        scripts = self.home_dir / ".agents/skills/agmsg/scripts"
        options_copy = self.temp_dir / "spawn-options.yaml"
        db = self.temp_dir / "messages.db"
        with sqlite3.connect(db) as connection:
            connection.execute(
                "CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS messages (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT, team TEXT, "
                "from_agent TEXT, to_agent TEXT, body TEXT, read_at TEXT)"
            )
        (scripts / "lib").mkdir(exist_ok=True)
        (scripts / "lib/validate.sh").write_text("agmsg_validate_team_name() { :; }\n")
        (scripts / "lib/storage.sh").write_text(f"agmsg_db_path() {{ printf '%s\\n' {db}; }}\n")
        # Like a worker, the PONG echoes the PING's task_id (or pong_task_id).
        pong_insert = (
            'tid="$(sed -n \'s/.*task_id=\\([^ ]*\\).*/\\1/p\' <<< "$5")"\n'
            + (f'tid="{pong_task_id}"\n' if pong_task_id else "")
            + f"""sqlite3 {db} "INSERT INTO messages (team, from_agent, to_agent, body) VALUES ('$1', '$3', '$2', 'AGMSG-PONG v1 task_id=$tid status=alive note=x');"\n"""
            if pong
            else ""
        )
        dispatch = self.bin_dir / "agmsg-dispatch"
        dispatch.write_text(
            f"""#!/usr/bin/env bash
printf 'agmsg-dispatch %s\\n' "$*" >> {self.calls_path}
[[ {dispatch_exit} -eq 0 ]] || {{ printf 'agmsg-dispatch: fake wake refused\\n' >&2; exit {dispatch_exit}; }}
sqlite3 {db} "INSERT INTO messages (team, from_agent, to_agent, body, read_at) VALUES ('$1', '$2', '$3', '$5', '2026-10-01T00:00:00Z');"
{pong_insert}"""
        )
        dispatch.chmod(0o755)
        for name, body in {
            "spawn.sh": f"""printf 'spawn %s ws=%s\\n' "$*" "${{HERDR_WORKSPACE_ID:-}}" >> {self.calls_path}
printf 'spawn-socket %s\\n' "${{HERDR_SOCKET_PATH:-}}" >> {self.calls_path}
cp "$AGMSG_SPAWN_OPTIONS_FILE" {options_copy}
printf '%s\\n' '{json.dumps({"result": {"panes": [{"pane_id": pane} for pane in spawn_panes]}})}' > {self.pane_list_path}
""",
            "despawn.sh": f"""printf 'despawn %s\\n' "$*" >> {self.calls_path}
if [[ " $* " == *" --force "* ]]; then
    exit {force_exit}
fi
printf '%s\\n' '{despawn_output}'
exit {despawn_exit}
""",
            "leave.sh": f"""printf 'leave %s\\n' "$*" >> {self.calls_path}
""",
        }.items():
            (scripts / name).write_text("#!/usr/bin/env bash\n" + body)
            (scripts / name).chmod(0o755)
        return options_copy

    def add_seat_worktree(self, name: str) -> Path:
        path = self.workdir.resolve() / ".claude/worktrees" / name
        subprocess.run(
            ["git", "-C", str(self.workdir), "worktree", "add", "-q", "--detach", str(path), "origin/main"],
            check=True,
            capture_output=True,
        )
        return path

    def find_spec(self, fullname, target=None):
        """Try to find a spec for the specified module.

        Returns the matching spec, or None if not found.
        """
        is_namespace = False
        tail_module = fullname.rpartition('.')[2]
        try:
            mtime = _path_stat(self.path or _os.getcwd()).st_mtime
        except OSError:
            mtime = -1
        if mtime != self._path_mtime:
            self._fill_cache()
            self._path_mtime = mtime
        # tail_module keeps the original casing, for __file__ and friends
        if _relax_case():
            cache = self._relaxed_path_cache
            cache_module = tail_module.lower()
        else:
            cache = self._path_cache
            cache_module = tail_module
        # Check if the module is the name of a directory (and thus a package).
        if cache_module in cache:
            base_path = _path_join(self.path, tail_module)
            for suffix, loader_class in self._loaders:
                init_filename = '__init__' + suffix
                full_path = _path_join(base_path, init_filename)
                if _path_isfile(full_path):
                    return self._get_spec(loader_class, fullname, full_path, [base_path], target)
            else:
                # If a namespace package, return the path if we don't
                #  find a module in the next section.
                is_namespace = _path_isdir(base_path)
        # Check for a file w/ a proper suffix exists.
        for suffix, loader_class in self._loaders:
            try:
                full_path = _path_join(self.path, tail_module + suffix)
            except ValueError:
                return None
            _bootstrap._verbose_message('trying {}', full_path, verbosity=2)
            if cache_module + suffix in cache:
                if _path_isfile(full_path):
                    return self._get_spec(loader_class, fullname, full_path,
                                          None, target)
        if is_namespace:
            _bootstrap._verbose_message('possible namespace for {}', base_path)
            spec = _bootstrap.ModuleSpec(fullname, None)
            spec.submodule_search_locations = [base_path]
            return spec
        return None

codex
[PR #408](https://github.com/mryfmo/dotfiles/pull/408), audited at `bb84bdefde6eef6ca23297e0ae3c6fef050b630f` against the specified base.

- **[P1] High confidence — `home/dot_local/bin/common/executable_herdr-agents:1834`**: The new `python3 -c 'import fcntl; …'` runs from the repository directory, allowing a repository-controlled `fcntl.py` or `fcntl/__init__.py` to execute with the host operator’s authority before cap enforcement; the unlock at line 1901 repeats this exposure.
- **[P2] High confidence — `.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-worker-cap-hookspath-a02/task.md:25`**: The claimed replacement control does not hold: the audited head retains both task copies, including the predecessor’s contradictory threat-table claims about Git configuration writes.

For P1, local interpreter checks confirmed that the working directory is searched first and `fcntl` is neither built in nor already loaded. Isolate both Python invocations, for example with `-I`, and add a regression containing a hostile repository-local module.

The threat-table assessment is:

| Controls | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Manifest and SKILL | Value four and matching documentation are present. |
| Launcher, in-process effects, interpreter/environment | Ordinary count-to-registration locking is supported by the code; host execution remains exposed through P1. |
| Test fixtures | Temporary repositories, HOME and fake services provide isolation as written. |
| Task-copy replacement | Fails: P2. |
| Branch artifacts, evidence records, metadata | Dispatch hashes, provenance and acceptance checks cannot be verified from the supplied changeset. |
| Metadata separators | Front matter is bounded correctly; dispatched byte identity remains unverified. |
| New or renamed scripts | None introduced. |
| Merge and update-branch effects | Procedural controls; their execution is not evidenced by this diff. |
| Forced removal | No removal change introduced. |

No additional introduced secret disclosure, unsafe deserialization, shell injection or path-traversal issue was identified. The cap uses the deployed manifest, and the diff introduces no Git configuration writes.

📝 まとめ: Security review completed with one host-execution vulnerability and one threat-table inconsistency; acceptance remains with the orchestrator.

Not run: unit/Bats tests or live Herdr; GitHub CI was unavailable because `gh` could not connect. The host-execution risk remains unresolved.
